{"success": true, "name": "hermes-agent", "description": "Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.", "tags": ["hermes", "setup", "configuration", "multi-agent", "spawning", "cli", "gateway", "development"], "related_skills": ["claude-code", "codex", "opencode"], "content": "---\nname: hermes-agent\ndescription: \"Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.\"\nversion: 2.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent + Teknium\nlicense: MIT\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [hermes, setup, configuration, multi-agent, spawning, cli, gateway, development]\n    homepage: https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent\n    related_skills: [claude-code, codex, opencode]\n---\n\n# Hermes Agent\n\nHermes Agent is an open-source AI agent framework by Nous Research that runs in your terminal, messaging platforms, and IDEs. It belongs to the same category as Claude Code (Anthropic), Codex (OpenAI), and OpenClaw — autonomous coding and task-execution agents that use tool calling to interact with your system. Hermes works with any LLM provider (OpenRouter, Anthropic, OpenAI, DeepSeek, local models, and 15+ others) and runs on Linux, macOS, and WSL.\n\nWhat makes Hermes different:\n\n- **Self-improving through skills** — Hermes learns from experience by saving reusable procedures as skills. When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. Skills accumulate over time, making the agent better at your specific tasks and environment.\n- **Persistent memory across sessions** — remembers who you are, your preferences, environment details, and lessons learned. Pluggable memory backends (built-in, Honcho, Mem0, and more) let you choose how memory works.\n- **Multi-platform gateway** — the same agent runs on Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Email, and 10+ other platforms with full tool access, not just chat.\n- **Provider-agnostic** — swap models and providers mid-workflow without changing anything else. Credential pools rotate across multiple API keys automatically.\n- **Profiles** — run multiple independent Hermes instances with isolated configs, sessions, skills, and memory.\n- **Extensible** — plugins, MCP servers, custom tools, webhook triggers, cron scheduling, and the full Python ecosystem.\n\nPeople use Hermes for software development, research, system administration, data analysis, content creation, home automation, and anything else that benefits from an AI agent with persistent context and full system access.\n\n**This skill helps you work with Hermes Agent effectively** — setting it up, configuring features, spawning additional agent instances, troubleshooting issues, finding the right commands and settings, and understanding how the system works when you need to extend or contribute to it.\n\n**Docs:** https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/\n\n## WHEN TO USE\n\n- **Setting up or reconfiguring Hermes** — model, provider, gateway, tools, permissions\n- **Troubleshooting** — auth failures, tool errors, gateway connection issues, crashes\n- **Extending Hermes** — adding custom tools, plugins, skills, MCP servers, cron jobs\n- **Managing profiles** — creating, cloning, syncing, exporting/importing profiles\n- **Understanding Hermes internals** — how sessions, memory, skills, and toolsets work\n- **Contributing to Hermes** — reporting bugs, submitting PRs, running tests\n- **Configuration queries** — \"How do I set up X?\", \"Where is the config?\", \"Why is Y happening?\"\n\n## QUICK REFERENCE\n\n```bash\n# Essential commands\nhermes              # Interactive chat\nhermes setup        # Setup wizard\nhermes model        # Change model/provider\nhermes config edit  # Edit config.yaml\nhermes doctor       # Health check\nhermes tools        # Enable/disable toolsets\nhermes skills list  # List installed skills\nhermes profile list # List profiles\nhermes cron list    # List cron jobs\nhermes gateway run  # Start gateway\n```\n\n## Architecture Overview\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    Hermes Agent                             │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│                                                             │\n│  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────┐   │\n│  │ CLI      │  │ Gateway  │  │ Cron     │  │ ACP      │   │\n│  │ (hermes) │  │ (Telegram│  │ (cron)   │  │ (IDE)    │   │\n│  │          │  │  etc.)   │  │          │  │          │   │\n│  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘  └────┬─────┘   │\n│       │              │              │              │         │\n│       └──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────┘         │\n│                      ▼              ▼                       │\n│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐       │\n│  │              Agent Core                         │       │\n│  │  • Skill Loader  • Memory Manager               │       │\n│  │  • Tool Dispatcher • Session Manager            │       │\n│  │  • Context Compressor • Credential Pool          │       │\n│  └────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘       │\n│                       ▼                                    │\n│  ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────┐      │\n│  │ terminal │ │ file     │ │ web      │ │ browser  │      │\n│  │          │ │ search/  │ │ search/  │ │ automation│      │\n│  │          │ │ write    │ │ fetch    │ │          │      │\n│  └──────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────┘      │\n│                                                             │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│  Profiles: ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/                       │\n│  Config: ~/.hermes/config.yaml                              │\n│  Skills: ~/.hermes/skills/ + ~/.hermes/profiles/<name>/...  │\n│  Sessions: ~/.hermes/sessions/                              │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n## INTEGRATION POINTS\n\n| Related Skill | How They Connect |\n|---------------|-----------------|\n| **claude-code** | Claude Code runs as an external agent via Hermes terminal. Use `terminal()` to invoke, `pty=true` for interactive mode, `background=true` for long tasks. |\n| **codex** | OpenAI Codex runs the same way — `terminal()` with either print mode (`-p`) or PTY mode. Cron jobs can trigger codex workflows. |\n| **opencode** | Provider-agnostic coding agent. Same invocation pattern via `terminal()`. Useful for multi-provider comparisons. |\n| **ruflo** | Ruflo is the orchestrator layer above Hermes. Hermes provides the terminal/execution environment; ruflo coordinates multi-agent swarms. |\n| **openharness** | OpenHarness provides the ohmo persona system. SOUL.md can be injected into Hermes via `--append-system-prompt-file`. |\n| **nomos** | NOMOS workflows can invoke Hermes via terminal. Use Hermes as the execution engine within NOMOS state machines. |\n| **armory** | Armory skills can be installed via `hermes skills install`. Install armory skill packages into `~/.hermes/skills/` or profile skill dirs. |\n| **caveman** | Token compression for Hermes output. Add to CLAUDE.md or agent system prompt for compressed responses. |\n| **hermes-agent** | This skill — the authoritative reference for configuring and extending Hermes itself. |\n\n## PITFALLS & GOTCHAS\n\n1. **Config changes require a fresh session** — Most config settings (`security.redact_secrets`, `approvals.mode`, toolsets) are snapshotted at import time. To apply changes, run `hermes config set KEY VAL` then start a new session (`/reset` in chat or `hermes` again in CLI). This is deliberate — it prevents an LLM from flipping the toggle on itself mid-task.\n\n2. **Secret redaction is OFF by default** — Tool output passes through unmodified. If you need API keys and secrets masked, run `hermes config set security.redact_secrets true` and restart. This does NOT affect `approvals.mode` — they are independent settings.\n\n3. **YOLO mode is NOT safe for secrets** — Setting `approvals.mode: off` or `--yolo` disables command approval but does NOT disable secret redaction. These are independent controls.\n\n4. **Toolset changes don't apply mid-conversation** — Enabling/disabling toolsets takes effect on `/reset` (new session) only. They do NOT apply mid-conversation to preserve prompt caching. Tell users this explicitly.\n\n5. **Cron jobs run in a fresh session** — Cron cron-run sessions have no current-chat context. Prompts must be fully self-contained. Skills and context_from are the only way to inject prior state.\n\n6. **Script-based cron jobs expect scripts in the profile's scripts/ directory** — Not `~/.hermes/scripts/`. The scheduler looks for scripts at `~/.hermes/profiles/<profile>/scripts/<name>.sh`.\n\n7. **tmux sessions persist after Claude Code exits** — If Claude Code crashes or is killed mid-session, the tmux session remains. Always clean up with `tmux kill-session -t <name>` when done.\n\n8. **Gateway auth requires browser** — OAuth verification codes pasted via tmux `send-keys` fail because tmux treats them as password input. The user must log in on their local machine with a browser for initial auth.\n\n9. **Print mode can't write files** — Claude Code in `-p` mode refuses file writes. Use shell redirect (`> output.md 2>&1`) or the `write_file` tool instead.\n\n10. **Fable 5 session token budget** — Fable 5 has a per-session token budget. Running multiple parallel Fable 5 jobs can exhaust the budget mid-run. Run them sequentially or with max 2 concurrent.\n\n11. **Session resumption requires same directory** — `--continue` only works in the same working directory. For cross-directory work, capture the session ID and resume explicitly.\n\n12. **Context degradation is real** — AI output quality measurably degrades above 70% context window usage. Monitor with `/context` and proactively `/compact`.\n\n13. **`/exit` opens agent selector in OpenCode** — It's NOT a valid exit command. Use Ctrl+C to exit OpenCode sessions.\n\n14. **OpenCode needs `pty=true` for interactive mode** — OpenCode is a TUI app. Without PTY, it hangs. `opencode run` (bounded tasks) does NOT need PTY.\n\n15. **OAuth tokens are stored in `~/.hermes/auth.json`** — If auth is failing, check this file. Credential pools rotate automatically but can get stuck — use `hermes auth reset PROVIDER` to clear exhaustion.\n\n## Quick Start\n\n```bash\n# Install\ncurl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash\n\n# Interactive chat (default)\nhermes\n\n# Single query\nhermes chat -q \"What is the capital of France?\"\n\n# Setup wizard\nhermes setup\n\n# Change model/provider\nhermes model\n\n# Check health\nhermes doctor\n```\n\n---\n\n## CLI Reference\n\n### Global Flags\n\n```\nhermes [flags] [command]\n\n  --version, -V             Show version\n  --resume, -r SESSION      Resume session by ID or title\n  --continue, -c [NAME]     Resume by name, or most recent session\n  --worktree, -w            Isolated git worktree mode (parallel agents)\n  --skills, -s SKILL        Preload skills (comma-separate or repeat)\n  --profile, -p NAME        Use a named profile\n  --yolo                    Skip dangerous command approval\n  --pass-session-id         Include session ID in system prompt\n```\n\nNo subcommand defaults to `chat`.\n\n### Chat\n\n```\nhermes chat [flags]\n  -q, --query TEXT          Single query, non-interactive\n  -m, --model MODEL         Model (e.g. anthropic/claude-sonnet-4)\n  -t, --toolsets LIST       Comma-separated toolsets\n  --provider PROVIDER       Force provider (openrouter, anthropic, nous, etc.)\n  -v, --verbose             Verbose output\n  -Q, --quiet               Suppress banner, spinner, tool previews\n  --checkpoints             Enable filesystem checkpoints (/rollback)\n  --source TAG              Session source tag (default: cli)\n```\n\n### Configuration\n\n```\nhermes setup [section]      Interactive wizard (model|terminal|gateway|tools|agent)\nhermes model                Interactive model/provider picker\nhermes config               View current config\nhermes config edit          Open config.yaml in $EDITOR\nhermes config set KEY VAL   Set a config value\nhermes config path          Print config.yaml path\nhermes config env-path      Print .env path\nhermes config check         Check for missing/outdated config\nhermes config migrate       Update config with new options\nhermes login [--provider P] OAuth login (nous, openai-codex)\nhermes logout               Clear stored auth\nhermes doctor [--fix]       Check dependencies and config\nhermes status [--all]       Show component status\n```\n\n### Tools & Skills\n\n```\nhermes tools                Interactive tool enable/disable (curses UI)\nhermes tools list           Show all tools and status\nhermes tools enable NAME    Enable a toolset\nhermes tools disable NAME   Disable a toolset\n\nhermes skills list          List installed skills\nhermes skills search QUERY  Search the skills hub\nhermes skills install ID    Install a skill (ID can be a hub identifier OR a direct https://…/SKILL.md URL; pass --name to override when frontmatter has no name)\nhermes skills inspect ID    Preview without installing\nhermes skills config        Enable/disable skills per platform\nhermes skills check         Check for updates\nhermes skills update        Update outdated skills\nhermes skills uninstall N   Remove a hub skill\nhermes skills publish PATH  Publish to registry\nhermes skills browse        Browse all available skills\nhermes skills tap add REPO  Add a GitHub repo as skill source\n```\n\n### MCP Servers\n\n```\nhermes mcp serve            Run Hermes as an MCP server\nhermes mcp add NAME         Add an MCP server (--url or --command)\nhermes mcp remove NAME      Remove an MCP server\nhermes mcp list             List configured servers\nhermes mcp test NAME        Test connection\nhermes mcp configure NAME   Toggle tool selection\n```\n\n### Gateway (Messaging Platforms)\n\n```\nhermes gateway run          Start gateway foreground\nhermes gateway install      Install as background service\nhermes gateway start/stop   Control the service\nhermes gateway restart      Restart the service\nhermes gateway status       Check status\nhermes gateway setup        Configure platforms\n```\n\nSupported platforms: Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Email, SMS, Matrix, Mattermost, Home Assistant, DingTalk, Feishu, WeCom, BlueBubbles (iMessage), Weixin (WeChat), API Server, Webhooks. Open WebUI connects via the API Server adapter.\n\nPlatform docs: https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/user-guide/messaging/\n\n### Sessions\n\n```\nhermes sessions list        List recent sessions\nhermes sessions browse      Interactive picker\nhermes sessions export OUT  Export to JSONL\nhermes sessions rename ID T Rename a session\nhermes sessions delete ID   Delete a session\nhermes sessions prune       Clean up old sessions (--older-than N days)\nhermes sessions stats       Session store statistics\n```\n\n### Cron Jobs\n\n```\nhermes cron list            List jobs (--all for disabled)\nhermes cron create SCHED    Create: '30m', 'every 2h', '0 9 * * *'\nhermes cron edit ID         Edit schedule, prompt, delivery\nhermes cron pause/resume ID Control job state\nhermes cron run ID          Trigger on next tick\nhermes cron remove ID       Delete a job\nhermes cron status          Scheduler status\n```\n\nFor script-only cron jobs, prefer `no_agent=true` and put the executable script or wrapper in the active profile's `scripts/` directory; the cron tool expects a relative script filename, not an absolute path. For backing up a live Hermes profile/home Git repository such as `~/.hermes`, verify the live repository path rather than creating a separate clone. See `references/profile-repo-hourly-git-sync.md`.\n\n### Webhooks\n\n```\nhermes webhook subscribe N  Create route at /webhooks/<name>\nhermes webhook list         List subscriptions\nhermes webhook remove NAME  Remove a subscription\nhermes webhook test NAME    Send a test POST\n```\n\n### Profiles\n\n```\nhermes profile list         List all profiles\nhermes profile create NAME  Create (--clone, --clone-all, --clone-from)\nhermes profile use NAME     Set sticky default\nhermes profile delete NAME  Delete a profile\nhermes profile show NAME    Show details\nhermes profile alias NAME   Manage wrapper scripts\nhermes profile rename A B   Rename a profile\nhermes profile export NAME  Export to tar.gz\nhermes profile import FILE  Import from archive\n```\n\nWhen syncing a profile `SOUL.md` from a GitHub agent identity file or installing companion skills from direct URLs, preserve exact source bytes and use non-interactive install flags (`--category`, `--yes`). See `references/profile-soul-sync-and-external-skill-install.md`.\n\n### Credential Pools\n\n```\nhermes auth add             Interactive credential wizard\nhermes auth list [PROVIDER] List pooled credentials\nhermes auth remove P INDEX  Remove by provider + index\nhermes auth reset PROVIDER  Clear exhaustion status\n```\n\n### Other\n\n```\nhermes insights [--days N]  Usage analytics\nhermes update               Update to latest version\nhermes pairing list/approve/revoke  DM authorization\nhermes plugins list/install/remove  Plugin management\nhermes honcho setup/status  Honcho memory integration (requires honcho plugin)\nhermes memory setup/status/off  Memory provider config\nhermes completion bash|zsh  Shell completions\nhermes acp                  ACP server (IDE integration)\nhermes claw migrate         Migrate from OpenClaw\nhermes uninstall            Uninstall Hermes\n```\n\n---\n\n## Slash Commands (In-Session)\n\nType these during an interactive chat session.\n\n### Session Control\n```\n/new (/reset)        Fresh session\n/clear               Clear screen + new session (CLI)\n/retry               Resend last message\n/undo                Remove last exchange\n/title [name]        Name the session\n/compress            Manually compress context\n/stop                Kill background processes\n/rollback [N]        Restore filesystem checkpoint\n/background <prompt> Run prompt in background\n/queue <prompt>      Queue for next turn\n/resume [name]       Resume a named session\n```\n\n### Configuration\n```\n/config              Show config (CLI)\n/model [name]        Show or change model\n/personality [name]  Set personality\n/reasoning [level]   Set reasoning (none|minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh|show|hide)\n/verbose             Cycle: off → new → all → verbose\n/voice [on|off|tts]  Voice mode\n/yolo                Toggle approval bypass\n/skin [name]         Change theme (CLI)\n/statusbar           Toggle status bar (CLI)\n```\n\n### Tools & Skills\n```\n/tools               Manage tools (CLI)\n/toolsets            List toolsets (CLI)\n/skills              Search/install skills (CLI)\n/skill <name>        Load a skill into session\n/cron                Manage cron jobs (CLI)\n/reload-mcp          Reload MCP servers\n/plugins             List plugins (CLI)\n```\n\n### Gateway\n```\n/approve             Approve a pending command (gateway)\n/deny                Deny a pending command (gateway)\n/restart             Restart gateway (gateway)\n/sethome             Set current chat as home channel (gateway)\n/update              Update Hermes to latest (gateway)\n/platforms (/gateway) Show platform connection status (gateway)\n```\n\n### Utility\n```\n/branch (/fork)      Branch the current session\n/fast                Toggle priority/fast processing\n/browser             Open CDP browser connection\n/history             Show conversation history (CLI)\n/save                Save conversation to file (CLI)\n/paste               Attach clipboard image (CLI)\n/image               Attach local image file (CLI)\n```\n\n### Info\n```\n/help

[Subdirectory context discovered: .hermes/hermes-agent/AGENTS.md]
# Hermes Agent - Development Guide

Instructions for AI coding assistants and developers working on the hermes-agent codebase.

**Never give up on the right solution.**

## What Hermes Is

Hermes is a personal AI agent that runs the same agent core across a CLI, a
messaging gateway (Telegram, Discord, Slack, and ~20 other platforms), a TUI,
and an Electron desktop app. It learns across sessions (memory + skills),
delegates to subagents, runs scheduled jobs, and drives a real terminal and
browser. It is extended primarily through **plugins and skills**, not by
growing the core.

Two properties shape almost every design decision and are the lens for
reviewing any change:

- **Per-conversation prompt caching is sacred.** A long-lived conversation
  reuses a cached prefix every turn. Anything that mutates past context,
  swaps toolsets, or rebuilds the system prompt mid-conversation invalidates
  that cache and multiplies the user's cost. We do not do it (the one
  exception is context compression).
- **The core is a narrow waist; capability lives at the edges.** Every model
  tool we add is sent on every API call, so the bar for a new *core* tool is
  high. Most new capability should arrive as a CLI command + skill, a
  service-gated tool, or a plugin — not as core surface.

## Contribution Rubric — What We Want / What We Don't

This is the project's intent layer. Use it two ways:

1. **For humans and for your own work** — what gets merged and what gets
   rejected, so a contribution aims at the target.
2. **For automated review (the triage sweeper)** — guidance on when a PR is
   safe to close on the three allowed reasons (`implemented_on_main`,
   `cannot_reproduce`, `incoherent`) and, just as important, **when NOT to
   close** one. Taste-based "we don't want this / out of scope" closes are NOT
   an automated decision — those stay with a human maintainer. The sweeper's
   job here is to recognize design intent and *avoid wrongly closing a
   legitimate contribution*, not to make the won't-implement call itself.

Read the balance right: Hermes ships a **lot** — most merges are bug fixes to
real reported behavior, and the product surface (platforms, channels,
providers, models, desktop/TUI features) expands aggressively and on purpose.
The restraint below is aimed squarely at the **core agent + the model tool
schema**, the one place where every addition is paid for on every API call.
"Smallest footprint" governs *how a capability is wired into the core*, NOT
whether the product is allowed to grow. We are expansive at the edges and
conservative at the waist.

### What we want

- **Fix real bugs, well.** The bulk of what lands is `fix(...)` against an
  actual reported symptom. A good fix reproduces the symptom on current
  `main`, points to the exact line where it manifests, and fixes the whole bug
  class — sibling call paths included — not just the one site the reporter hit.
- **Expand reach at the edges.** New platform adapters, channels, providers,
  models, and desktop/TUI/dashboard features are welcome and land routinely,
  including large ones (a new messaging channel, a session-cap feature, a
  Windows PTY bridge). Breadth in the product is a goal, not a footprint
  concern — as long as it integrates with the existing setup/config UX
  (`hermes tools`, `hermes setup`, auto-install) rather than bolting on a raw
  env var.
- **Refactor god-files into clean modules.** Extracting a multi-thousand-line
  cluster out of `cli.py` / `run_agent.py` / `gateway/run.py` into a focused
  mixin or module is wanted work, even when the diff is huge and mechanical
  (large `+N/-N` refactors merge regularly). The "every line traces to the
  request" test applies to *feature* PRs; a declared refactor's request IS the
  extraction.
- **Keep the core narrow.** New *model tools* are the expensive exception —
  every tool ships on every API call. Prefer, in order: extend existing code →
  CLI command + skill → service-gated tool (`check_fn`) → plugin → MCP server
  in the catalog → new core tool (last resort). See "The Footprint Ladder."
- **Extend, don't duplicate.** Before adding a module/manager/hook, check
  whether existing infrastructure already covers the use case. When several PRs
  integrate the same *category*, design one shared interface instead of merging
  them one at a time (see the ABC + orchestrator note under the Footprint
  Ladder).
- **Behavior contracts over snapshots.** Tests should assert how two pieces of
  data must relate (invariants), not freeze a current value (model lists,
  config version literals, enumeration counts). See "Don't write
  change-detector tests."
- **E2E validation, not just green unit mocks.** For anything touching
  resolution chains, config propagation, security boundaries, remote
  backends, or file/network I/O, exercise the real path with real imports
  against a temp `HERMES_HOME`. Mocks hide integration bugs.
- **Cache-, alternation-, and invariant-safe.** Preserve prompt caching, strict
  message role alternation (never two same-role messages in a row; never a
  synthetic user message injected mid-loop), and a system prompt that is
  byte-stable for the life of a conversation.
- **Contributor credit preserved.** Salvage external work by cherry-picking
  (rebase-merge) so authorship survives in git history; don't reimplement from
  scratch when you can build on top.

### What we don't want (rejected even when well-built)

- **Speculative infrastructure.** Hooks, callbacks, or extension points with no
  concrete consumer. Adding a hook is easy; removing one after plugins depend
  on it is hard. A hook is NOT speculative if a contributor has a real, stated
  use case — even if the consumer ships separately.
- **New `HERMES_*` env vars for non-secret config.** `.env` is for secrets
  only (API keys, tokens, passwords). All behavioral settings — timeouts,
  thresholds, feature flags, display prefs — go in `config.yaml`. Bridge to an
  internal env var if the mechanism needs one, but user-facing docs point to
  `config.yaml`. Reject PRs that tell users to "set X in your .env" unless X
  is a credential.
- **A new core tool when terminal + file already do the job, or when a skill
  would.** If the only barrier is file visibility on a remote backend, fix the
  mount, not the toolset.
- **Lazy-reading escape hatches on instructional tools.** No `offset`/`limit`
  pagination on tools that load content the agent must read fully (skills,
  prompts, playbooks). Models will read page 1 and skip the rest.
- **"Fixes" that destroy the feature they secure.** A mitigation that kills the
  feature's purpose is the wrong mitigation. Read the original commit's intent
  (`git log -p -S`) before restricting behavior; find a fix that preserves the
  feature.
- **Outbound telemetry / usage attribution without opt-in gating.** No new
  analytics, third-party identifier tagging, or attribution tags until a
  generic user-facing opt-in (config gate + setup prompt + `hermes tools`
  toggle) exists. Park behind a label, do not merge.
- **Change-detector tests, cache-breaking mid-conversation, dead code wired in
  without E2E proof, and plugins that touch core files.** Plugins live in their
  own directory and work within the ABCs/hooks we provide; if a plugin needs
  more, widen the generic plugin surface, don't special-case it in core.
- **Third-party products / other people's projects integrated into the core
  tree.** Observability backends, vendor SaaS integrations, analytics dashboards,
  and similar "someone else's product" plugins do NOT land under `plugins/` in
  this repo. They place an ongoing maintenance burden on us to keep them working
  against a fast-moving core, for a backend we don't own. Ship them as a

### [2026-09-14T07:27:52.263956+00:00] tool · tool: terminal

{"output": "The cloud providers listed in [Other Compatible Providers](#other-compatible-providers) all speak OpenAI's REST dialect, so they wire up the same way under `custom_providers:`. Three worked recipes follow. Each drops into `~/.hermes/config.yaml` and the matching API key goes in `~/.hermes/.env`.\n\n#### Together AI\n\nHosts open-weight models (Llama, MiniMax, Gemma, DeepSeek, Qwen) at prices significantly below first-party APIs. Good default for multi-model fleets.\n\n```yaml\n# ~/.hermes/config.yaml\ncustom_providers:\n  - name: together\n    base_url: https://api.together.xyz/v1\n    key_env: TOGETHER_API_KEY\n    # api_mode: chat_completions  # default — no need to set\n\nmodel:\n  default: MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M2.7   # or any model from together.ai/models\n  provider: custom:together\n```\n\n```bash\n# ~/.hermes/.env\nTOGETHER_API_KEY=your-together-key\n```\n\nSwitch models mid-session:\n\n```\n/model custom:together:meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct-Turbo\n/model custom:together:google/gemma-4-31b-it\n/model custom:together:deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V3\n```\n\nTogether's `/v1/models` endpoint works, so `hermes model` can auto-discover available models.\n\n#### Groq\n\nUltra-fast inference (~500 tok/s on Llama-3.3-70B). Small catalog but strong for latency-sensitive interactive use.\n\n```yaml\n# ~/.hermes/config.yaml\ncustom_providers:\n  - name: groq\n    base_url: https://api.groq.com/openai/v1\n    key_env: GROQ_API_KEY\n\nmodel:\n  default: llama-3.3-70b-versatile\n  provider: custom:groq\n```\n\n```bash\n# ~/.hermes/.env\nGROQ_API_KEY=your-groq-key\n```\n\n#### Perplexity\n\nUseful when you want a model that does live web search and citation automatically. Strict about which models are available — check [perplexity.ai/settings/api](https://www.perplexity.ai/settings/api) for the current list.\n\n```yaml\n# ~/.hermes/config.yaml\ncustom_providers:\n  - name: perplexity\n    base_url: https://api.perplexity.ai\n    key_env: PERPLEXITY_API_KEY\n\nmodel:\n  default: sonar\n  provider: custom:perplexity\n```\n\n```bash\n# ~/.hermes/.env\nPERPLEXITY_API_KEY=your-perplexity-key\n```\n\n#### Multiple providers in one config\n\nThe three recipes compose — use all of them together and switch per turn with `/model custom:<name>:<model>`:\n\n```yaml\ncustom_providers:\n  - name: together\n    base_url: https://api.together.xyz/v1\n    key_env: TOGETHER_API_KEY\n  - name: groq\n    base_url: https://api.groq.com/openai/v1\n    key_env: GROQ_API_KEY\n  - name: perplexity\n    base_url: https://api.perplexity.ai\n    key_env: PERPLEXITY_API_KEY\n\nmodel:\n  default: MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-M2.7\n  provider: custom:together      # boot to Together; switch freely after\n```\n\n:::tip Troubleshooting\n- `hermes doctor` should print no `Unknown provider` warnings for any of these names after the CLI validator fixes in #15083.\n- If a provider's `/v1/models` endpoint is unreachable (Perplexity is the common one), `hermes model` will persist the model with a warning rather than hard-reject — see #15136.\n- To skip `custom_providers:` entirely and use bare `provider: custom` with `CUSTOM_BASE_URL` env var, see #15103.\n:::\n\n---", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

GET params must go in the querystring for PVE\n            path = path + ('&' if '?' in path else '?') + enc.decode()\n        else:\n            data = enc\n    if method != 'GET':\n        headers['CSRFPreventionToken'] = csrf\n    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + path, data=data, headers=headers, method=method)\n    try:\n        resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=180)\n        return json.load(resp)\n    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:\n        if e.code == 401 and retry:\n            _login()\n            return api(method, path, body, retry=False)\n        raise\n\n\ndef find_vm(node_hint=None, vmid=None, ip=None):\n    \"\"\"Locate a VM/LXC by vmid or current IP (via guest agent if possible).\"\"\"\n    vms = []\n    nodes = [n['node'] for n in api('GET', '/api2/json/nodes')['data']]\n    for node in nodes:\n        for vm in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu')['data']:\n            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': vm['vmid'], 'name': vm.get('name', ''), 'status': vm['status'], 'type': 'qemu'})\n        for ct in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/lxc')['data']:\n            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': ct['vmid'], 'name': ct.get('name', ''), 'status': ct['status'], 'type': 'lxc'})\n    return vms", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "# LLM-MANAGER-v0.11-API-MODEL-ROUTING.md\n\n**Date:** 2026-09-13 · **System:** MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager v0.11.0-default-baseline (VM114 / 10.0.20.108)\n\n## 1. Client contract\n\n```text\nOPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1\nOPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>\n```\n\n- `GET /v1/models` → models currently routable **for any valid key** (all hosted models, §E1).\n- `POST /v1/chat/completions` with explicit `model` (logical name or role alias).\n- Missing `model` → request validation error. Unknown model → explicit 400 (LiteLLM \"Invalid model name passed in model=...\"), never silent substitution.\n- Keys do not encode endpoints, do not pin deployments, and carry no default model. API base shown in the UI is a constant string.\n\n## 2. Routing data flow (single source of truth)\n\n```text\nhosts (desired_power_state, management_mode)          [per-VM policy]\n  └─ model_registry (health, routable, context_limit) ← sync_registry() every 60s\n        └─ litellm_sync.py regenerates /etc/llm-manager/litellm_config.yaml\n              └─ LiteLLM routes exact `model` → deployment (api_base)\n                    └─ routing_logger callback records model + api_base + tokens + latency\n                       into request_routing_log (per-request deployment attribution §D4.4)\n```\n\n## 3. Routability gate (§C2)\n\nA model is routable iff ALL of:\n\n```text\nhosts.desired_power_state = RUNNING\nhosts.management_mode = MANAGED\nbackend /v1/models returns 200\nbackend health completion (1 token) returns 200\nmodel_registry.routable = TRUE\n```\n\nGated `/v1/models` (nginx `location = /v1/models` → FastAPI :8001) omits non-routable models entirely (§C3 preference for omission). All other `/v1/*` traffic goes to LiteLLM :4000.\n\n## 4. Role aliases (§22)\n\n| Alias | Target | Status at report time |\n|---|---|---|\n| `fast` | qwen3.6-35b-a3b | routable (multiple healthy backends; alias pinned to primary backend .161) |\n| `code` | qwen3.8-27b | NOT routable — target not yet qualified (Phase 20) |\n| `frontier` | frontier model | NOT routable (Phase 21) |\n\nAliases are ordinary explicit model strings; they create no default model. `auto` is reserved but NOT activated (§22).\n\n## 5. Verified request-level evidence (§D4)\n\nLive `request_routing_log` rows through the manager:\n\n| model | api_base resolved | tokens (p/c) | latency |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| startupteams/llamacpp | http://10.0.20.165:8000/v1/ | 11 / 8 | 603 ms |\n\nPhysical deployment attribution is recorded per request. The client never sees backend IPs.\n\n## 6. Deployment pinning check (§L)\n\n- No deployment-pin API exists in client surface. Physical mapping is server-side only.\n- Verified: agent key works across any hosted model by changing `model` in the request body.\n\n## 7. Intentional-off behavior (§K3)\n\nWhen `desired_power_state = STOPPED_INTENTIONAL`:\n\n```text\nmodel_registry.routable → FALSE (sync_registry runs on state change)\ngated /v1/models omits affected models only if no healthy deployment remains\nrecovery engine logs intentional_state_pending (info) — never power-ons, never counted as outage\nactual VM stop requires separate explicit /power-off call (409 unless intentional-off set)\n```\n\n## 8. Known limitations\n\n- Alias→backend selection currently picks the first routable backend (sorted by IP); HA within alias waits for per-deployment load weighting (future work, out of v0.11 scope).\n=== ssh config ===\n/home/jordatech/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "=== fallback_providers doc ===\nConfigure a chain of backup providers Hermes tries in order when the primary model fails (rate limits, server errors, auth failures). The canonical format is a top-level `fallback_providers:` list:\n\n```yaml\nfallback_providers:\n  - provider: openrouter\n    model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-4\n  - provider: anthropic\n    model: claude-sonnet-4\n    # base_url: http://localhost:8000/v1    # optional, for custom endpoints\n    # api_mode: chat_completions           # optional override\n```\n\nThe legacy single-pair `fallback_model:` dict is still accepted for back-compat:\n\n```yaml\nfallback_model:\n  provider: openrouter\n  model: anthropic/claude-sonnet-4\n```\n\nWhen activated, the fallback swaps the model and provider mid-session without losing your conversation. The chain is tried entry-by-entry; activation is one-shot per session.\n\nSupported providers: `openrouter`, `nous`, `novita`, `openai-codex`, `copilot`, `copilot-acp`, `anthropic`, `gemini`, `qwen-oauth`, `huggingface`, `zai`, `kimi-coding`, `kimi-coding-cn`, `minimax`, `minimax-cn`, `minimax-oauth`, `deepseek`, `nvidia`, `xai`, `xai-oauth`, `ollama-cloud`, `bedrock`, `azure-foundry`, `opencode-zen`, `opencode-go`, `kilocode`, `xiaomi`, `arcee`, `gmi`, `stepfun`, `lmstudio`, `alibaba`, `alibaba-coding-plan`, `tencent-tokenhub`, `custom`.\n\n:::tip\nFallback is configured exclusively through `config.yaml` — or interactively via `hermes fallback`. For full details on when it triggers, how the chain advances, and how it interacts with auxiliary tasks and delegation, see [Fallback Providers](/user-guide/features/fallback-providers).\n:::\n\n---\n\n## See Also\n\n- [Configuration](/user-guide/configuration) — General configuration (directory structure, config precedence, terminal backends, memory, compression, and more)\n- [Environment Variables](/reference/environment-variables) — Complete reference of all environment variables", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"status": "success", "output": "VM103@miam00111: FAIL HTTP Error 596: Broken pipe\n\nVM401@miam00112: FAIL HTTP Error 596: Broken pipe\n\n--- guest_exec source for reference ---\ndef guest_exec(node, vmid, command, timeout=120):\n    \"\"\"Run command in guest via qemu-guest-agent, wait for completion, return (stdout, stderr, exitcode).\n    PVE expects 'command' as repeated form fields (arg encode_array).\"\"\"\n    # PVE array convention: repeat the parameter name with each value\n    # NOTE: 'timeout' is NOT an accepted param for agent/exec (400s); the exec returns a pid\n    parts = []\n    for c in command:\n        parts.append('command=' + urllib.parse.quote(c, safe=''))\n    body = '&'.join(parts).encode()\n    url = PVE + f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec'\n    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}', 'CSRFPreventionToken': csrf,\n               'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded'}\n    req = urllib.request.Request(url, data=body, headers=headers, method='POST')\n    resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=timeout + 30)\n    pdata = json.load(resp)['data']\n    pid = pdata['pid'] if isinstance(pdata, dict) else pdata\n    import time\n    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n    while time.time() < deadline:\n        time.sleep(2)\n        try:\n            st = api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec-status', {'pid': pid})['data']\n        except Exception:\n            continue\n        if st.get('exited'):\n            return st.get('out-data', ''), st.get('err-data', ''), st.get('exitcode', -1)\n    return '', 'TIMEOUT', -1\n\n", "tool_calls_made": 0, "duration_seconds": 10.39}

{"output": "runtime_provider = runtime.get(\"provider\", \"\")\n    runtime_model = str(runtime.get(\"model\") or \"\")\n    runtime_base_url = str(runtime.get(\"base_url\") or \"\")\n    runtime_api_key = runtime.get(\"api_key\", \"\")\n    runtime_api_mode = str(runtime.get(\"api_mode\") or \"\")\n\n    # Fall back to process-local globals when main_runtime dict was not\n    # provided or was incomplete.  ``set_runtime_main()`` now records\n    # base_url/api_key/api_mode alongside provider/model, so custom:\n    # providers get the full credential surface in Step 1 of the\n    # auto-detect chain.\n    if not runtime_base_url and _RUNTIME_MAIN_BASE_URL:\n        runtime_base_url = _RUNTIME_MAIN_BASE_URL\n    if not runtime_api_key and _RUNTIME_MAIN_API_KEY:\n        runtime_api_key = _RUNTIME_MAIN_API_KEY\n    if not runtime_api_mode and _RUNTIME_MAIN_API_MODE:\n        runtime_api_mode = _RUNTIME_MAIN_API_MODE\n\n    # ── Warn once if OPENAI_BASE_URL is set but config.yaml uses a named\n    #    provider (not 'custom'). {"output": "def try_activate_fallback(agent, reason: \"FailoverReason | None\" = None) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Switch to the next fallback model/provider in the chain.\n\n    Called when the current model is failing after retries.  Swaps the\n    OpenAI client, model slug, and provider in-place so the retry loop\n    can continue with the new backend.  Advances through the chain on\n    each call; returns False when exhausted.\n\n    Uses the centralized provider router (resolve_provider_client) for\n    auth resolution and client construction — no duplicated provider→key\n    mappings.\n    \"\"\"\n    if reason in {FailoverReason.rate_limit, FailoverReason.billing}:\n        # Only start cooldown when leaving the primary provider.  If we're\n        # already on a fallback and chain-switching, the primary wasn't the\n        # source of the 429 so the cooldown should not be reset/extended.\n        fallback_already_active = bool(getattr(agent, \"_fallback_activated\", False))\n        current_provider = (getattr(agent, \"provider\", \"\") or \"\").strip().lower()\n        primary_provider = ((agent._primary_runtime or {}).get(\"provider\") or \"\").strip().lower()\n        if (not fallback_already_active) or (primary_provider and current_provider == primary_provider):\n            agent._rate_limited_until = time.monotonic() + 60\n    if agent._fallback_index >= len(agent._fallback_chain):\n        # Chain exhausted.  If we actually walked a non-empty chain and the\n        # failure was NOT a rate-limit/billing event (those already armed\n        # their own 60s cooldown above), arm a short cooldown so the next\n        # turn's restore_primary_runtime stays gated instead of resetting\n        # _fallback_index=0 and re-marshaling the whole context across every\n        # provider again.  Guards the cross-turn replay storm in #24996.\n        if (\n            len(agent._fallback_chain) > 0\n            and reason not in {FailoverReason.rate_limit, FailoverReason.billing}\n        ):\n            _existing_cooldown = getattr(agent, \"_rate_limited_until\", 0) or 0\n            agent._rate_limited_until = max(\n                _existing_cooldown,\n                time.monotonic() + _FALLBACK_EXHAUSTED_COOLDOWN_S,\n            )\n        return False\n    fb = agent._fallback_chain[agent._fallback_index]\n    agent._fallback_index += 1\n    fb_provider = (fb.get(\"provider\") or \"\").strip().lower()\n    fb_model = (fb.get(\"model\") or \"\").strip()\n    if not fb_provider or not fb_model:\n        return agent._try_activate_fallback()  # skip invalid, try next\n\n    # Skip entries that resolve to the current (provider, model) — falling\n    # back to the same backend that just failed loops the failure. Compare\n    # base_url too so two distinct custom_providers entries pointing at the\n    # same shim/proxy URL also dedup. See issue #22548.\n    current_provider = (getattr(agent, \"provider\", \"\") or \"\").strip().lower()\n    current_model = (getattr(agent, \"model\", \"\") or \"\").strip()\n    current_base_url = str(getattr(agent, \"base_url\", \"\") or \"\").rstrip(\"/\").lower()\n    fb_base_url_for_dedup = (fb.get(\"base_url\") or \"\").strip().rstrip(\"/\").lower()\n    if fb_provider == current_provider and fb_model == current_model:\n        logger.warning(\n            \"Fallback skip: chain entry %s/%s matches current provider/model\",\n            fb_provider, fb_model,\n        )\n        return agent._try_activate_fallback()\n    if (\n        fb_base_url_for_dedup\n        and current_base_url\n        and fb_base_url_for_dedup == current_base_url\n        and fb_model == current_model\n    ):\n        logger.warning(\n            \"Fallback skip: chain entry base_url %s matches current backend\",\n            fb_base_url_for_dedup,\n        )\n        return agent._try_activate_fallback()\n\n    # Use centralized router for client construction.\n    # raw_codex=True because the main agent needs direct responses.stream()\n    # access for Codex providers.\n    try:\n        from agent.auxiliary_client import resolve_provider_client\n        # Pass base_url and api_key from fallback config so custom\n        # endpoints (e.g. Ollama Cloud) resolve correctly instead of\n        # falling through to OpenRouter defaults.\n        fb_base_url_hint = (fb.get(\"base_url\") or \"\").strip() or None\n        fb_api_key_hint = (fb.get(\"api_key\") or \"\").strip() or None\n        if not fb_api_key_hint:\n            # key_env and api_key_env are both documented aliases (see\n            # _normalize_custom_provider_entry in hermes_cli/config.py).\n            fb_key_env = (fb.get(\"key_env\") or fb.get(\"api_key_env\") or \"\").strip()\n            if fb_key_env:", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "@staticmethod\n    def _compute_threshold_tokens(\n        context_length: int, threshold_percent: float, max_tokens: int | None = None,\n    ) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Compute the compaction trigger threshold in tokens.\n\n        The base value is ``effective_input_budget * threshold_percent``, floored\n        at ``MINIMUM_CONTEXT_LENGTH`` so large-context models don't compress\n        prematurely at 50%. BUT that floor degenerates at small windows: for a\n        model whose ``context_length`` is at/below the minimum (e.g. a 64K\n        local model), ``max(0.5*64000, 64000) == 64000`` makes the threshold\n        equal the ENTIRE window — auto-compression can never fire because the\n        provider rejects the request before usage reaches 100% (#14690).\n\n        When the floor would meet or exceed the context window, trigger at\n        ``_MIN_CTX_TRIGGER_RATIO`` (85%) of the window — high enough that a\n        small model uses most of its context before compacting, but below\n        100% so compaction fires before the provider rejects the request.\n\n        The provider reserves ``max_tokens`` of output space out of the same\n        window, so the usable INPUT budget is ``context_length - max_tokens``.\n        With a large ``max_tokens`` (e.g. 65536 on a custom provider) the input\n        budget is materially smaller than the raw window, and a threshold based\n        on the full window lets the session hit a provider 400 before compaction\n        fires (#43547). The percentage and the degenerate-window check below both\n        operate on the effective input budget. ``max_tokens=None`` (provider\n        default) conservatively assumes no reservation (full window).\n        \"\"\"\n        effective_window = context_length - (max_tokens or 0)\n        if effective_window <= 0:\n            effective_window = context_length\n        pct_value = int(effective_window * threshold_percent)\n        floored = max(pct_value, MINIMUM_CONTEXT_LENGTH)\n        # If flooring pushed the threshold to/over the effective window it can\n        # never be reached. Trigger at 85% of the effective input budget so a\n        # minimum-context model rides most of its budget before compacting\n        # instead of wasting half.\n        if effective_window > 0 and floored >= effective_window:\n            return max(1, min(int(effective_window * ContextCompressor._MIN_CTX_TRIGGER_RATIO),\n                              effective_window - 1))\n        return floored\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        model: str,\n        threshold_percent: float = 0.50,\n        protect_first_n: int = 3,\n        protect_last_n: int = 20,\n        summary_target_ratio: float = 0.20,\n        quiet_mode: bool = False,\n=== update_model threshold ===\n662:    def update_model(\n684:        self.threshold_tokens = self._compute_threshold_tokens(\n689:        target_tokens = int(self.threshold_tokens * self.summary_target_ratio)\n742:    def _compute_threshold_tokens(\n831:        # for models right at the minimum. _compute_threshold_tokens also\n834:        self.threshold_tokens = self._compute_threshold_tokens(\n840:        target_tokens = int(self.threshold_tokens * self.summary_target_ratio)\n851:                model, self.context_length, self.threshold_tokens,\n915:            if self.last_prompt_tokens < self.threshold_tokens:\n933:        if rough_tokens < self.threshold_tokens:", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

The full picture is now verified from source. Design decision:

- **This key is an LLM Manager (LiteLLM) virtual key** — the sanctioned client contract (`llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1`, auth-enforced, per-request routing attribution). Direct backend IPs don't even enforce auth, so the router is the correct surface.
- **Hard blocker for main-model use:** Hermes enforces `MINIMUM_CONTEXT_LENGTH = 64_000` at agent init (hard-coded, no override), and the vLLM backends currently serve `max_model_len: 32768` / llama.cpp 8192. Verified: no config escape hatch — a smaller reported window is rejected at init.
- **Works today:** local models as `fallback_providers` entries (fallback activation path skips the floor check; the compressor auto-triggers at 85% of a small window, so a 32K fallback is safe).

### [2026-09-14T08:05:34.962708+00:00] assistant

All done and verified end-to-end. Here's the full picture:

## ✅ Hermes ↔ Local Models: Wired & Tested

**What your key actually is:** a LiteLLM virtual key for the **LLM Manager router** (`https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1`) — not a bare-backend key. That's the sanctioned client contract: auth-enforced (401 on bad key), per-request routing attribution, health-gated. Direct backend IPs don't even check auth, so I routed everything through the router.

### Configuration (profile `config.yaml` + `.env`, key staged 0600, never echoed)

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Custom provider | `custom:marion` → router (TLS self-signed cert installed into the venv trust bundle, backup kept) |
| Models declared | `qwen3.6-35b-a3b` (262K), `qwen3.8-27b`/`code` (32K), `fast` (32K), `startupteams/llamacpp` (8K) |
| `model.max_tokens` | 16384 — the built-in custom-provider default (65536) overflowed the 32K backends with 400s |
| Fallback chain | OpenRouter (primary) → `qwen3.6-35b-a3b` → `qwen3.8-27b` — if OpenRouter fails, you fail over to local automatically |

### Server fix made along the way
**VM401 / .164** was the real unit `vllm-qwen.service` and was missing `--enable-auto-tool-choice --reasoning-parser qwen3 --tool-call-parser qwen3_xml` — it 400'd every tool-calling request (~⅓ of router traffic for the qwen3.6 pool). Patched, restarted, verified tool calls now pass. Backup of the unit left in place.

### Verified E2E (real runs, not probes)
- `hermes chat` on `qwen3.6-35b-a3b` via router → answers ✓
- Full **tool loop** (local model calling Hermes terminal tools) → `MARION-LOCAL-E2E-PASS` ✓

### One constraint to know about
Hermes hard-floors the **main** model at 64K context (no override exists). Today only `qwen3.6-35b-a3b` passes — `.163`/`.164` run `--max-model-len auto` = 262K. `.161` (qwen3.8) and `.162` are pinned at 32K, so those only work as fallbacks or after a server restart. If you want qwen3.8 as a main model, the fix is: bump `--max-model-len 32768 → 65536` in .161's launch preset via the LLM Manager hosting UI, restart the service. Say the word and I'll do it.

### Two flags for you
1. **LLM Manager routing_logger is silently dropping rows** — callback's registered and importable, but recent traffic doesn't land in `request_routing_log`. Existing v0.11 observability bug, worth a look.
2. **Gateway restart needed** to apply the new config to Telegram sessions (can't restart itself from inside). Run: `systemctl --user restart hermes-gateway-agent_stea004_entrepreneur` — CLI runs (`hermes chat -m custom:marion:...`) already work immediately.

Also saved `hermes-local-model-setup` as a skill documenting all of this (including the `hermes config set` stores-lists-as-strings trap).
